 \paragraph{Statistical and training-seed blocks.} The statistical bootstrap draws independent Poisson weights of mean one for each event in data, signal simulation, and background simulation. Each simulated signal event receives a single draw shared by its truth and reconstruction records. These factors are propagated through the full unfolding, including the background treatment and the cross-section extraction. Independent Poisson counts are the large-sample limit of the multinomial bootstrap counts for any fixed subset of events.  \paragraph{Run-period and prior-shape terms.} The run-period term uses three separately unfolded groups. For each group we divide the unfolded result bin by bin by that group's simulated truth, subtract the exposure-weighted mean of the three ratios, and multiply the resulting deviations by the pooled central cross section. The covariance is formed from the outer products of these three displacement vectors about that reference. The same construction is used on the sixteen-cell integrals, scaling the ratio deviations to the pooled integrals to propagate correlated changes across cells. The resulting matrices have rank at most two. The spread includes the finite statistics of the individual periods, with no subtraction for their sampling noise. The construction was chosen after the period differences were observed (Appendix~\ref{app:choices-after-results}).
 
-For prior-shape sensitivity, the simulation prior is reweighted in turn using the shapes of GENIE with Valencia MEC, NuWro, and GiBUU relative to nominal GENIE, and the data are unfolded again. If $s_g$ is the resulting displacement from the central value for reweighting $g$, we form $C_{\mathrm{model}}=\sum_g s_gs_g^{\mathsf T}$, with no ensemble-size divisor. The reweightings preserve the prior's normalization. We give this term separately from $Q_R$ and include it in the sixteen-cell covariance [Eq.~\eqref{eq:sixteen-cell-covariance}]. It describes sensitivity to these shape changes and has no assigned sampling distribution.

-\paragraph{Sixteen-cell covariance.} The sixteen-cell block $C_e$ combines a refitted statistical bootstrap with flux, interaction-model, detector, and lateral-band covariances and the correlated target normalization. The refit includes the negative-weight background refinement. Training is deterministic in this configuration, so the seed term is zero. The response block $C_{\mathrm{resp}}$ is a sum of four outer products. For each deposit category (protons, mesons, electromagnetic showers, and other deposits), the vector is half the difference between the unfolded results after shifting that category's reconstructed energy up and down by one standard deviation. These shifts are made separately and their outer products are added to $C_e+C_{\mathrm{model}}$ and the run-period term as specified in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sixteen-cell-covariance}. We form the component matrices in the space of cell integrals. In particular, the response vector for a deposit category is obtained by aggregating each shifted unfolding to the sixteen regions before taking the half-difference. This retains correlations among regions and permits coherent propagation to sums of cells. For a generator comparison we add the prediction's diagonal finite-sample covariance, with entries given by the sum of squared event weights in each region after converting the weights to cross-section units.

Discarding directions changes the residual being tested, so the cutoff is part of the definition of the statistic.  A multidimensional quadratic distance is quoted with its covariance, inverse and cutoff, retained rank, and sensitivity to rank truncation. The retained rank is the dimension of the statistic. In particular, projection cannot increase the rank of a covariance. For each sample covariance used in an inverse one must also specify its ensemble size and normalization, the inverted dimension, and any correction for finite-ensemble bias. For $N$ independent Gaussian draws and a fixed $p$-dimensional subspace, the inverse-bias factor is $(N-p-2)/(N-1)$ with $1/(N-1)$ covariance normalization, or $(N-p-2)/N$ with $1/N$ normalization, for $N>p+2$. These assumptions limit its use for a sum of systematic bands or a subspace selected from the same covariance spectrum. The sum of mean-centered band covariances has rank at most the sum of the individual ranks, and each band contributes at most $N_b-1$ independent directions. An independent normalization outer product can add at most one direction.

 For the principal results, we give each data event unit weight and append the simulated background with the negative of its exposure-scaled weights. If $D(x)$ and $B(x)$ are the selected data and background densities in reconstructed coordinates $x$, the signed measured class targets the first-step ratio $[D(x)-B(x)]/S(x)$ where the net density is positive, with $S(x)$ the current weighted density of selected simulated signal.
 
-Before the first OmniFold classifier is trained, we convert the signed sample to nonnegative weights. A separate classifier, trained with absolute event weights, estimates the probability $g(x)$ that an event in the signed sample has positive weight. The original weight $w$ is replaced by $|w|\max\{0,2g(x)-1\}$~\cite{Nachman:2025staypositive}. The absolute-weight sample has density $D+B$. In the population limit $g=D/(D+B)$, so multiplying this density by the clipped factor gives the positive part of $D-B$. The learned factor implements this prescription without a subtraction grid. Some checks instead subtract the background with binned purity, weighting data by the estimated signal fraction in each reconstructed bin. We label these purity-subtracted (Appendix~\ref{app:negative-weight-background}).

 \subsection{Closure tests}
 \label{sec:muon-cross-section-validation}
 \label{sec:validation}
 
-All closure tests use the same detector simulation as the unfolding, and their conclusions apply to the specific injected shapes and amplitudes. The signal-only closure tests recover the injected $p_T$ and $p_\parallel$ features to median absolute fractional residuals of $0.046\%$ and $0.013\%$, respectively, inside a $1.5\%$ threshold (\hyperlink{validation-2d-closure}{closure rows} of Table~\ref{tab:validation-inventory}, which lists each threshold as descriptive). The purity-subtracted tests use five iterations (Appendix~\ref{app:negative-weight-background}) and simulation from one run period. A $17.2\%$ Gaussian truth feature leaves a $1.69\%$ residual, 0.098 of the injection, which passes the feature-recovery check of Ref.~\cite{Canelli:2025guide}. Reweighting the pseudo-data by a quasielastic axial-mass variation or a flux variation gives residuals of $0.068\%$ and $0.376\%$, respectively, inside a $2\%$ threshold, against references extrapolated from the accepted signal. These references probe shape changes conditional on the simulated acceptance.

inputs/appD_reproducibility.tex:20:\phantomsection\label{app:release-scope} The deposit also archives the \lrval{q-ncells}-cell 2D refined central values with $\CrepTwoD$ and its systematic, statistical, training-seed and run-period blocks, and the 2D prior-shape term $C_{\mathrm{model}}$, which $\CrepTwoD$ excludes; the sixteen refined cell integrals with $\CrepFiveD$ and its components, with $C_{\mathrm{model}}$ listed separately; the generator predictions compared in this article on the sixteen cells and on the $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ grid; and the central values and support masks of the \resFiveAxis{}, with the projection matrix $P$ and the available-energy--mass projection covariance $C_{EW}$. Apart from the replay package, it contains no code that regenerates this article's tables or figures. The deposit starts from derived inputs. Rebuilding the closure, bias, or covariance-validation results, or extracting statistics from the archived pseudo-experiment products, requires additional inputs. Covariances of other projections of the five-axis fine grid require the full five-axis covariance $C_Z$, which is not deposited. An event-level rerun requires the MINERvA AnaTuples and generator samples, plus the event-loop products or the inputs to regenerate them. The deposit identifies the 2,374 input files by SHA-256, including 2,307 AnaTuples and 67 flux, reweighting, and parameter files~\cite{AnaTuplePreservation2026}. The preserved inputs are outside the public deposit. The ancillary files of the published MINERvA measurement supply its reference values, bin mapping, and covariance for the 2D comparison~\cite{MINERvA:2021owq}.
inputs/appD_reproducibility.tex:69:The matrices $\CrepTwoD$, $C_Z$, and $\CrepFiveD$ refer, respectively, to the \lrval{q-ncells}-bin muon result, the \resFiveAxis{}, and the sixteen refined cell integrals. The records document keeps the earlier symbols: $Q_R$ is $\CrepTwoD$, $C_{\mathrm{rep}}$ is $\CrepFiveD$, $C_{\mathrm{total}}$ is $\Csub$, $C_{\mathrm{PL,5D}}$ is the five-dimensional $\Crun$, and ``the baseline covariance'' is $\CrepFiveDzero$. Their constructions are given in Appendixes~\ref{app:covariance-construction} and \ref{app:multidimensional-statistics}. For the exploratory $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ density projection, the matrix $P$ sums over the three dropped axes with their bin widths. The covariance of the projected densities $y=Px$ is $C_{EW}=PC_ZP^{\mathsf T}$, with relative standard deviation $\sqrt{(C_{EW})_{rr}}/y_r$ in cell $r$. The deposit archives $C_{EW}$ and $P$, not $C_Z$. The spectrum of $C_{EW}$ extends to the double-precision noise floor, making inversion sensitive to regularization. To obtain cell integrals, each density is multiplied by its own $\Delta E_{\mathrm{avail}}\Delta W$, including the full width of each tail bin. The projection applies the fine-grid mask and uses no additional acceptance factor at the destination binning; whether one is needed has not been decided. The deposit's README gives the array ordering and a worked projection check.

inputs/sec5_multidim.tex:52:The covariance $\CrepFiveD$ combines the effects of varying the unfolding inputs ($C_e$, their statistical and systematic covariance, rescaled to $C_e^{\mathrm{infl}}$ as described below), the starting shape ($C_{\mathrm{model}}$), the response to energy deposits ($C_{\mathrm{resp}}$), and the spread across run periods ($\Crun$). inputs/sec5_multidim.tex:58:To obtain $C_e$, we repeat the unfolding with statistical resampling, flux, interaction-model and detector variations, including shifts to reconstructed observables and selection, and propagate target normalization. Training uses fixed seeds and all training rows to build tree histograms. The seed term is zero. The prior-shape term sums outer products of prior-reweighting shifts. The response term varies reconstructed energy separately for proton, meson, electromagnetic, and other deposits (Appendix~\ref{app:statistical-methods}). We multiply the eigenvalues of $C_e$ in ranks 1--4, 5--8 and 9--16 by \fvval{vf-phi-1}, \fvval{vf-phi-2} and \fvval{vf-phi-3}, respectively. Each factor estimates the trace of the pseudo-experiment covariance over that band of eigenvalue ranks divided by the trace of the rebuilt-reference covariance over the same band, and it multiplies the eigenvalues of $C_e$ in that band. Joint clipping and differences in statistical resampling can contribute to those ratios, and the rescaling also acts on sources absent from the pseudo-experiments, including lateral variations and normalization, so interval coverage remains unvalidated. The result is $C_e^{\mathrm{infl}}$. The subtotal $\Csub=C_e+C_{\mathrm{model}}$ keeps the unscaled $C_e$.

inputs/sec5_multidim.tex:106:In simulation without counting fluctuations, $\rho$ is the signed fraction of the shift along the prior-to-truth direction still missing after unfolding. The outcomes are consistent with a limit from estimating weights for missed events at the GiBUU truth and from how the truth-level classifier represents the change in momentum transfer at the tilt. The true weights are unknown for data. inputs/appA_methods.tex:18:The two-dimensional covariance $\CrepTwoD$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:muon-covariance-block-sum} is evaluated on the \lrval{q-ncells} reported cross-section densities. Each ensemble uses the same five-iteration procedure as the central value, with the input varied according to the source under study. The block sum neglects cross-covariances between sources and assumes that the changes from simultaneous input variations are additive. In particular, the statistical and systematic blocks use a fixed training seed, so their variation with the choice of seed is outside the block sum.
inputs/appA_methods.tex:60:For prior-shape sensitivity, the simulation prior is reweighted in turn using the shapes of GENIE with Valencia MEC, NuWro, and GiBUU relative to nominal GENIE, and the data are unfolded again. If $s_g$ is the resulting displacement from the central value for reweighting $g$, we form $C_{\mathrm{model}}=\sum_g s_gs_g^{\mathsf T}$, with no ensemble-size divisor. The reweightings preserve the prior's normalization. We give this term separately from $\CrepTwoD$ and include it in the sixteen-cell covariance [Eq.~\eqref{eq:sixteen-cell-covariance}]. It describes sensitivity to these shape changes and has no assigned sampling distribution.
inputs/appA_methods.tex:74:Formally full rank can still leave the two-dimensional distance dominated by poorly determined modes. The universe covariance retains 138 of 205 modes at $10^{-12}\lambda_{\max}$, and adding the bootstrap raises that count to 205 with a retained condition number of $7.95\times10^{11}$. For $\CrepTwoD$ the count is 198 at $10^{-10}\lambda_{\max}$. The direct inverse of $\CrepTwoD$ gives a quadratic distance of 21.96 per reported bin against the published central value, compared with \chiPaperOurEdges{} using the published covariance on the same $p_T$ edges. The latter has a nearly diagonal, full-rank statistical block that provides variance in all directions. For numerical comparisons based on $\CrepTwoD$ alone, the truncated inverse uses the positive modes above $10^{-10}\lambda_{\max}$. In that convention the distance is the sum of squared residual amplitudes along the retained eigenvectors divided by their eigenvalues. Discarding directions changes the residual being tested, so the cutoff is part of the definition of the statistic. The publication comparison instead quotes the number of reported bins as the denominator, as specified in Appendix~\ref{app:comparison-closure-coverage}.

inputs/appA_methods.tex:216:For unfolded integrals $F$ and predicted integrals $\mu_G$, the total statistic is $T_{\mathrm{tot}}=(F-\mu_G)^{\mathsf T}\Sigma^{-1}(F-\mu_G)$. The metric $\Sigma=V+\operatorname{diag}[\operatorname{Var}(\mu_G)]$ uses a Ledoit--Wolf shrinkage estimate~\cite{LedoitWolf:2004} $V$ from \jtVn{} MINERvA Tune v1 pilot pseudo-experiments and adds the prediction's diagonal finite-sample variance. For $T_{\mathrm{sh}}$ we use normalized cell fractions, propagate $\Sigma$ through the normalization Jacobian in Eq.~\eqref{eq:app-shape-jacobian}, and drop one redundant fraction. The metric sets the relative weights of the discrepancies. The reference distribution comes from rerunning the estimator with the same fixed metric. It incorporates estimator bias, including retention of the prior, which can shift the null away from a quadratic form centered at zero residual.

inputs/appC_supplementary.tex:243:where $d_i=(X_i-P_i)/P_i$ and $b_i=(\widehat X_i-X_i)/P_i$ for prior, true, and unfolded integrals $P_i$, $X_i$, and $\widehat X_i$. This gives $\rho=0$ for exact recovery, $\rho=1$ for staying at the prior, and $\rho<0$ for overshooting along that direction. On noise-free samples with background, $\rho=\fvval{prr-rho-e-gibuu}$ at the GiBUU hybrid and $\fvval{prr-rho-e-q3}$ at the $q_3$ tilt. Reducing classifier capacity or using only muon momenta increases retention at both truths. Dropping $W$ alone or dropping both $W$ and $q_3$ changes $\rho$ by less than \fvval{prr-s53-w-dmax}. None of the tested changes in iterations, capacity, training statistics, or inputs reduces retention by \fvval{prr-floor}. Under $\CrepFiveDzero$, none removes either truth's bias flag. A second prespecified study on the same noise-free samples found that the retention at the GiBUU hybrid is consistent with a limit set by the weights of missed events. At the $q_3$ tilt, it is consistent with a limit set by the representation in the second, truth-level step. lines=open(\"inputs/note.diff\").read().splitlines()
for i,s in enumerate(lines):
 if s.startswith(\"diff \"):
  f=s.split()[-1].rsplit(\"/\",1)[-1]
  print(\"\\n\"+f)
 if s.startswith(\"@@\"):
  n=int(re.search(r\"\\+(\\d+)\",s)[1])
 if s.startswith(\" \") or (s.startswith(\"+\") and not s.startswith(\"+++\")):
  if s.startswith(\"+\") and not lines[i-1].startswith(\"+\"):
   p=i
   while lines[p-1].startswith(\"-\"): p-=1
   j=i
   while j<len(lines) and lines[j].startswith(\"+\"): j+=1
   a=\" \".join(x[1:] for x in lines[p:i]).split()
   b=\" \".join(x[1:] for x in lines[i:j]).split()
   print(\" \",n,end=\" \")

    49	
    50	\phantomsection
    51	\label{sec:fived16-cov}
    52	The covariance $\CrepFiveD$ combines the effects of varying the unfolding inputs ($C_e$, their statistical and systematic covariance, rescaled to $C_e^{\mathrm{infl}}$ as described below), the starting shape ($C_{\mathrm{model}}$), the response to energy deposits ($C_{\mathrm{resp}}$), and the spread across run periods ($\Crun$). The prior-shape term $C_{\mathrm{model}}$ is included in $\CrepFiveD$, whereas the 2D result reports it beside $\CrepTwoD$ and does not include it.
    53	\begin{equation}
    54	  \CrepFiveD = C_e^{\mathrm{infl}}+C_{\mathrm{model}}+C_{\mathrm{resp}}+\Crun,
    55	  \qquad \Csub=C_e+C_{\mathrm{model}} .
    56	  \label{eq:sixteen-cell-covariance}
    57	\end{equation}
    58	To obtain $C_e$, we repeat the unfolding with statistical resampling, flux, interaction-model and detector variations, including shifts to reconstructed observables and selection, and propagate target normalization. Training uses fixed seeds and all training rows to build tree histograms. The seed term is zero. The prior-shape term sums outer products of prior-reweighting shifts. The response term varies reconstructed energy separately for proton, meson, electromagnetic, and other deposits (Appendix~\ref{app:statistical-methods}). We multiply the eigenvalues of $C_e$ in ranks 1--4, 5--8 and 9--16 by \fvval{vf-phi-1}, \fvval{vf-phi-2} and \fvval{vf-phi-3}, respectively. Each factor estimates the trace of the pseudo-experiment covariance over that band of eigenvalue ranks divided by the trace of the rebuilt-reference covariance over the same band, and it multiplies the eigenvalues of $C_e$ in that band. Joint clipping and differences in statistical resampling can contribute to those ratios, and the rescaling also acts on sources absent from the pseudo-experiments, including lateral variations and normalization, so interval coverage remains unvalidated. The result is $C_e^{\mathrm{infl}}$. The subtotal $\Csub=C_e+C_{\mathrm{model}}$ keeps the unscaled $C_e$.
    59	
    60	The reported covariance in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sixteen-cell-covariance} gives a median per-cell uncertainty of $\fvval{sigmed-reported}\%$. Flux and prior-shape variations give the largest median contributions. Shifts to reconstructed observables and the spread across run periods also contribute appreciably. Prior-shape sensitivity gives fractional standard deviations above $\fpeval{floor(min(\fvval{sig-C_model-R02},\fvval{sig-C_model-R03},\fvval{sig-C_model-R11}))}\%$ in R02, R03, and R11. Before adding the run-period term, adding the energy-response covariance to $C_e^{\mathrm{infl}}+C_{\mathrm{model}}$ increases the per-cell standard deviation by up to about \fvval{sigratio-infl-maxpct}\%. Appendix~\ref{app:higher-dimensional-projections} gives the cell-by-cell decomposition and the correlation matrix.
    61	
    62	\subsection{Projection consistency}
    63	\label{sec:sixteen-cell-projections}
    64	\label{sec:fived16-quadrants}
    65	
    66	Summing the sixteen-cell result over the hadronic coordinates gives four coarse muon integrals within $\fpeval{round(abs(\fvval{cr-QC-dpct}),1)}\%$ of the 2D OmniFold result. In the order of Table~\ref{tab:five-dimensional-regions}, the differences from 2D are $+\fpeval{round(\fvval{cr-QA-dpct},2)}\%$, $\fpeval{round(\fvval{cr-QB-dpct},2)}\%$, $\fpeval{round(\fvval{cr-QC-dpct},2)}\%$, and $+\fpeval{round(\fvval{cr-QD-dpct},2)}\%$. The largest fractional difference is in the high-$p_T$, low-$p_\parallel$ quadrant. The sum over all four quadrants is $\fpeval{round(100*(1-(\fvval{cr-QA-s16}+\fvval{cr-QB-s16}+\fvval{cr-QC-s16}+\fvval{cr-QD-s16})/(\fvval{cr-QA-s2}+\fvval{cr-QB-s2}+\fvval{cr-QC-s2}+\fvval{cr-QD-s2})),2)}\%$ below the 2D integral.
    67	
    68	All four differences meet the agreement rule in Appendix~\ref{app:higher-dimensional-projections}, which gives the assumed-correlation thresholds and the scan over cross-covariances. These sums are insensitive to redistributions among hadronic cells within a muon quadrant.
    69	
    85	\label{sec:closure-map}
    86	\label{sec:val-fiveaxis}
    87	
    88	\label{sec:3d-closure}
    89	A marginal sums over the other coordinates, so recovery of the marginal can hide redistribution among joint cells. We reweight simulated truth and reconstructed events, then compare the unfolded result with the reweighted truth using the simulated MINERvA response.
    90	
    91	The signal-only checks leave residuals of $1.84\%$ in the 3D $E_{\mathrm{avail}}$ marginal and at most $1.1\%$ in the 4D $q_3$ marginal for the tested smooth features. The $W$ marginal meets its recovery criterion, while the joint $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ cells near the peak remain $2.2\%$ to $9.4\%$ low and some injected strength appears in adjacent mass bins (Appendix~\ref{app:comparison-closure-coverage} and Table~\ref{tab:higher-dimensional-validation}).
    92	
    93	\label{sec:val-negweight}
    94	At nominal truth, negative-weight refinement removes most of the high-$W$ bias from purity subtraction and meets the closure criterion for the sixteen cells. Appendix~\ref{app:negative-weight-background} compares the background treatments in detail.
    95	
    96	\label{sec:val-departure}
    97	Recovery is weaker under alternative truths. Changing the true available-energy spectrum by the difference between two generator shapes leaves large joint residuals with a negative-weight refinement trained with fewer trees. The residuals persist when the test is repeated without background. Changing the conditional $q_3$ distribution while holding the $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ truth fixed also shifts the unfolded cells in that plane. The sixteen-cell estimator recovers only part of the generator-derived changes (Appendix~\ref{app:bias-prior-stability}).
    98	
    99	\subsubsection{Prior dependence}
   100	\label{sec:higher-dimensional-prior}
   101	\label{sec:fived16-fd}
   102	
   103	Under $\CrepFiveD$, the sixteen-cell estimator exceeds the bias criteria at \fvval{f0b-nlisted-word} of the \fvval{f0b-all-truths} simulated truths, the GiBUU hybrid truth, which takes its fine-bin integrals from GiBUU and the composition inside each bin from the MINERvA simulation. Under $\CrepFiveDzero$, the reported covariance with unscaled $C_e$ in place of $C_e^{\mathrm{infl}}$, the synthetic $q_3$ tilt also exceeded them. It changes the conditional momentum transfer at fixed $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$. Its bias is the same under both covariances, so the difference is the covariance's. The standardized residuals for the GiBUU truth exceed the per-cell threshold in R02 and R06, the two low-$p_T$ cells with high available energy, low momentum transfer, and low mass (Table~\ref{tab:five-dimensional-regions}). Appendix~\ref{app:bias-prior-stability} gives the bias criteria and statistics.
   104	
   105	\label{sec:fived16-prr}
   106	In simulation without counting fluctuations, $\rho$ is the signed fraction of the shift along the prior-to-truth direction still missing after unfolding. Thus $\rho=0$ means exact recovery and $\rho=1$ means no shift from the prior (Eq.~\eqref{eq:five-dimensional-retained-prior}). At the two truths flagged under $\CrepFiveDzero$, the result matches the reconstructed pseudo-data while $\rho$ remains \fvval{prr-rho-e-gibuu} for GiBUU and \fvval{prr-rho-e-q3} for the momentum-transfer tilt. These true-weight substitutions serve only as simulation diagnostics. The outcomes are consistent with a limit from estimating weights for missed events at the GiBUU truth and from how the truth-level classifier represents the change in momentum transfer at the tilt. The true weights are unknown for data. Changing inputs, learner capacity, training statistics, or iterations leaves both biases above the criteria under $\CrepFiveDzero$, and the GiBUU hybrid's under $\CrepFiveD$ (Appendix~\ref{app:bias-prior-stability}).
   107	
   108	\label{sec:gbdt-model-dependence}
   109	Coarsening leaves substantial prior dependence (Appendix~\ref{app:bias-prior-stability}). The prior-shape terms for the sixteen cells and the 2D result measure shifts under the tested priors and give no calibrated bound on bias.
   110	
   111	\subsubsection{Algorithm dependence}
   120	
   121	\subsubsection{Numerical stability}
   122	\label{sec:higher-dimensional-calibration}
   123	\label{sec:5d}
   124	
   125	Rounding-level changes to data inputs shift the sixteen-cell estimates by 0.8 at the median and up to \lrval{stab-max} statistical standard deviations. These shifts exceed both tolerances set before the test, \fvval{ens1-bar-median} at the median and \fvval{ens1-bar-max} at the maximum. We perturb stored inputs by up to half a single-precision unit in the last place with fine-bin membership fixed, and test the joint cells, $E_{\mathrm{avail}}$--$W$ integrals and their sum, and the total rate. Appendix~\ref{app:bias-prior-stability} gives the alternative settings and stricter pseudo-data comparison.
   126	
   127	\subsubsection{Closure at held-out tunes}
   128	\label{sec:closure-interval-diagnostics}
   129	
   130	Mean residuals and repeated containment test different properties. Separate Poisson and noise-free checks meet the mean-bias criteria at both held-out GENIE v3 tunes, with the largest cell bias below \fvval{f1-bsig-ceil} of its reported standard deviation. The joint interval shortfall leaves the relative contributions of bias and scatter unresolved. Neither $\CrepTwoD$ nor $\CrepFiveD$ includes a term estimated from these closures.
   131	
   132	\phantomsection\label{sec:sixteen-cell-closure-tunes} For the sixteen-cell cross section, the joint regions contain the truth in \fvval{ll5d-njoint-min} to \fvval{ll5d-njoint-max} of \fvval{ll5d-ntoys} draws across both tunes and band variants. The initial score uses $\Csub=C_e+C_{\mathrm{model}}$, with unscaled $C_e$ and without the response and run-period terms. These counts document an interval shortfall without calibrating coverage (Appendix~\ref{app:choices-after-results}).
   133	
   134	Using $\CrepFiveDzero$ on the same draws lowers the mean $\chi^2/\mathrm{ndf}$ from \fvval{rg-16-orig-min}--\fvval{rg-16-orig-max} to \fvval{rg-16-full-min}--\fvval{rg-16-full-max}. That covariance is still too small for these residuals. An end-to-end test would also need to draw the response and run-period variations, which were absent from these draws. Appendix~\ref{app:closure-interval-diagnostics} gives the joint and per-bin tests, including the separate undercoverage result for purity subtraction with fixed training settings.
   135	
   136	\subsection{Comparison with generator predictions}
   137	\label{sec:sixteen-cell-generator-comparison}
   138	\label{sec:fived16-gen}
   139	
   140	All three hydrocarbon predictions lie below the unfolded integrals in every high-$p_T$ cell. Figure~\ref{fig:sixteen-cell-generator-residuals} compares the sixteen integrals with GENIE 2.12.10~\cite{Andreopoulos:2009rq} without and with the Valencia meson-exchange current (MEC) contribution and with MINERvA Tune v1, the unfolding prior. We also compare with NuWro 21.09~\cite{Golan:2012wx} and GiBUU 2019~\cite{Buss:2011mx} on carbon. All predictions use the analysis definitions of $W$ and the muon angle. The generated GiBUU sample ends at $E_\nu=\fvval{cmodel-gibuu-enu}~\mathrm{GeV}$, so the sixteen-cell comparison with GiBUU uses the eight cells with $p_\parallel<\fvval{h2-mid-pz}~\mathrm{GeV}/c$, which contain about $\fpeval{round(100*(\fvval{cr-QA-s16}+\fvval{cr-QC-s16})/(\fvval{cr-QA-s16}+\fvval{cr-QB-s16}+\fvval{cr-QC-s16}+\fvval{cr-QD-s16}),0)}\%$ of the reported cross section. The data are normalized per nucleon of hydrocarbon (CH), so the carbon comparisons also differ in target. The GiBUU prior-shape variation instead extends to $p_\parallel<\fvval{cmodel-gibuu-pz-max}~\mathrm{GeV}/c$, and the calibrated joint test uses its own domain (Sec.~\ref{sec:joint5d}).
   141	
   142	\begin{figure}[!hbp]
   143	  \centering
   144	  % Series targets and the GiBUU cell count verified against inputs2/fived16_genresidual_values.json.
   145	  \includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/fived16_genresidual.pdf}
   146	  \caption{All three hydrocarbon predictions lie below the unfolded integrals in every high-$p_T$ cell. The points show the fractional differences $(g_i-f_i)/f_i$ between the predicted cell integrals $g_i$ and the unfolded cell integrals $f_i$, on hydrocarbon for GENIE and MINERvA Tune v1 and on carbon for NuWro and GiBUU (GiBUU on its eight cells). The gray bars show $\pm\sqrt{(\CrepFiveD)_{ii}}/f_i$ about zero using only the diagonal of $\CrepFiveD$, with correlations and $V_g$, the diagonal covariance from finite generator statistics, entering the distances in Table~\ref{tab:sixteen-cell-model-distances}. Vertical separators indicate the four muon quadrants in Table~\ref{tab:five-dimensional-regions}. The cell IDs match the hadronic ranges in Table~\ref{tab:five-dimensional-regions} and Fig.~\ref{fig:fived16-headline}. The points are offset horizontally within each cell. See Sec.~\ref{sec:sixteen-cell-closure-tunes} for the limits of these uncertainties.}
   147	  \label{fig:sixteen-cell-generator-residuals}
   148	\end{figure}
   149	
   150	GENIE without Valencia MEC is below the unfolded result by $\fpeval{abs(\fvval{g-gcv-gf-R08})}\%$ in R08 and $\fpeval{abs(\fvval{g-gcv-gf-R14})}\%$ in R14. Both have $p_T\geq\fvval{h2-mid-pt}~\mathrm{GeV}/c$, but R08 selects low available energy and low hadronic mass, while R14 selects high momentum transfer and high hadronic mass. Adding Valencia MEC reduces these deficits to $\fpeval{abs(\fvval{g-gmec-gf-R08})}\%$ and $\fpeval{abs(\fvval{g-gmec-gf-R14})}\%$. The added strength also brings the prediction closer to the central value in R00, from $\fpeval{abs(\fvval{g-gcv-gf-R00})}\%$ below to $\fpeval{abs(\fvval{g-gmec-gf-R00})}\%$ below, at low $p_T$, $E_{\mathrm{avail}}$, and $q_3$. In both low-$p_T$ cells with high $E_{\mathrm{avail}}$, low $q_3$, and low $W$ (R02 and R06), GENIE already exceeds the central values by $\fpeval{round(\fvval{g-gcv-gf-R02},0)}$--$\fpeval{round(\fvval{g-gcv-gf-R06},0)}\%$ and the extra strength increases the excess to $\fpeval{round(\fvval{g-gmec-gf-R02},0)}$--$\fpeval{round(\fvval{g-gmec-gf-R06},0)}\%$. MINERvA Tune v1 lies below the unfolded result in fourteen cells, with deficits of about $\fpeval{round((abs(\fvval{g-tune-gf-R08})+abs(\fvval{g-tune-gf-R14}))/2,0)}\%$ in R08 and R14.
   151	
   152	Table~\ref{tab:sixteen-cell-model-distances} summarizes the differences as quadratic distances. See Sec.~\ref{sec:sixteen-cell-closure-tunes} for the limits of the covariance used here. For the rate-and-shape comparison the residual is $\boldsymbol{f}-\boldsymbol{g}$, with metric given by the inverse of $\CrepFiveD+V_g$, where $V_g$ is the prediction's diagonal finite-sample covariance. The shape comparison uses unit-normalized cell fractions. Normalizing reduces the distance substantially for GiBUU on its eight-cell domain and more modestly for NuWro. For the two GENIE predictions and MINERvA Tune v1, large distances persist after normalization. Of the predictions evaluated over all sixteen cells, the GENIE configuration without Valencia MEC has the smallest distance for both comparisons, even though the addition improves the agreement in several individual cells.

     4	\subsection{Result package, reproduction limits, and bin edges}
     5	\label{app:result-package}
     6	\label{app:release}
     7	
     8	The replay package~\cite{ReleaseCandidate2026} in the Zenodo deposit~\cite{ZenodoDeposit2026} contains approximately \SI{20}{MB} of derived inputs, a README, SHA-256 checksums, and a verifier. It recomputes the joint generator tests reported in this article (statistics, exceedance counts, $p$ values, decisions, and robustness and power summaries), with and without the recovered pseudo-experiments, together with the matched coarse-projection comparison and the fine-grid refinement ratios. It regenerates the $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ excess map (Fig.~\ref{fig:eavailWexcess}) exactly and checks the quoted $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ generator ratios against the arrays plotted in Fig.~\ref{fig:eavailWband}. It also recomputes the two-dimensional benchmark numbers of Sec.~\ref{sec:part-2d} for the \estTwoD{}, from the deposited central values and covariance and the published values and covariance. The replay package's README gives its file inventory, the figures it regenerates, and the verification tolerances.

    20	\phantomsection\label{app:release-scope} The deposit also archives the \lrval{q-ncells}-cell 2D refined central values with $\CrepTwoD$ and its systematic, statistical, training-seed and run-period blocks, and the 2D prior-shape term $C_{\mathrm{model}}$, which $\CrepTwoD$ excludes; the sixteen refined cell integrals with $\CrepFiveD$ and its components, with $C_{\mathrm{model}}$ listed separately; the generator predictions compared in this article on the sixteen cells and on the $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ grid; and the central values and support masks of the \resFiveAxis{}, with the projection matrix $P$ and the available-energy--mass projection covariance $C_{EW}$. Apart from the replay package, it contains no code that regenerates this article's tables or figures. The deposit starts from derived inputs. Rebuilding the closure, bias, or covariance-validation results, or extracting statistics from the archived pseudo-experiment products, requires additional inputs. Covariances of other projections of the five-axis fine grid require the full five-axis covariance $C_Z$, which is not deposited. An event-level rerun requires the MINERvA AnaTuples and generator samples, plus the event-loop products or the inputs to regenerate them. The deposit identifies the 2,374 input files by SHA-256, including 2,307 AnaTuples and 67 flux, reweighting, and parameter files~\cite{AnaTuplePreservation2026}. The preserved inputs are outside the public deposit. The ancillary files of the published MINERvA measurement supply its reference values, bin mapping, and covariance for the 2D comparison~\cite{MINERvA:2021owq}.

    48	\subsection{Code}
    49	\label{app:analysis-code}
    50	\label{app:codebase}
    51	
    52	The analysis code is available in the \href{https://github.com/josephbaileyy/MINERvA-OmniFold}{MINERvA OmniFold repository}. The MINERvA 101 event loop (\path{runEventLoopOmniFold.cpp}) writes per-run-period ROOT trees for data, simulated backgrounds, reconstructed signal, and fiducial true signal. These trees carry event and universe weights and are accompanied by the exposure and flux inputs. The 2D and 3D drivers (\path{unfold_2d_omnifold_unbinned.py} and \path{unfold_3d_omnifold_unbinned.py}) configure the features, selections, and normalization for their samples. The scalar 4D and 5D production code is under \path{nd-unfolding/}. The iterative reweighting is implemented in \path{unbinned_unfolding/python/omnifold.py}. The repository documents the upstream dependencies. The deposit links the release of the repository from which it was made, and its manifests record the digests of the replay package's and the five-axis products' inputs.
    53	% Cite the repository tag or commit once it exists.
    54	
    55	\begin{sloppypar}
    56	To replay the archived results, unpack the replay package from the deposit and run its verifier, \texttt{code/\allowbreak verify\_rc.py} (\texttt{publication/\allowbreak release/\allowbreak verify\_rc.py} in the repository), from its top directory with the pinned NumPy, SciPy, and Matplotlib requirements. A successful replay prints \verb|VERIFY: PASS|.
    57	\end{sloppypar}
    58	
    59	\subsection{Terminology and estimator definitions}
    60	\label{app:reproducibility-definitions}
    61	\label{sec:front-definitions}
    62	\label{app:devhistory-method}
    63	\label{app:devhistory-seeds}
    64	\label{app:devhistory-5d}
    65	\label{app:devhistory-recovery-seeds}
    66	
    67	Table~\ref{tab:estimators} gives the settings for each principal result. The configurations use five OmniFold iterations, with 100 trees, at most eight leaves, and learning rate 0.1 in the nominal boosted-tree learners. The \estTwoD{} uses seeded LightGBM (central seed 42), as does the \estPurity{} in two dimensions. The \estMultiD{} and the two deterministic controls construct LightGBM histograms from all training rows. Both reported refined estimators use a 400-tree, 31-leaf classifier for negative-weight refinement, while the low-capacity control uses 100 trees and eight leaves for that step. The \estExact{} uses scikit-learn gradient-boosted trees with exact splits and binned purity weights. Appendix~\ref{app:joint-generator-tests} specifies the variant used for the joint generator tests. The low-capacity recovery test and the refined-estimator tests at three withheld truth shapes reuse seed sets from estimator development.
    68	
    69	The matrices $\CrepTwoD$, $C_Z$, and $\CrepFiveD$ refer, respectively, to the \lrval{q-ncells}-bin muon result, the \resFiveAxis{}, and the sixteen refined cell integrals. The records document keeps the earlier symbols: $Q_R$ is $\CrepTwoD$, $C_{\mathrm{rep}}$ is $\CrepFiveD$, $C_{\mathrm{total}}$ is $\Csub$, $C_{\mathrm{PL,5D}}$ is the five-dimensional $\Crun$, and ``the baseline covariance'' is $\CrepFiveDzero$. Their constructions are given in Appendixes~\ref{app:covariance-construction} and \ref{app:multidimensional-statistics}. For the exploratory $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ density projection, the matrix $P$ sums over the three dropped axes with their bin widths. The covariance of the projected densities $y=Px$ is $C_{EW}=PC_ZP^{\mathsf T}$, with relative standard deviation $\sqrt{(C_{EW})_{rr}}/y_r$ in cell $r$. The deposit archives $C_{EW}$ and $P$, not $C_Z$. The spectrum of $C_{EW}$ extends to the double-precision noise floor, making inversion sensitive to regularization. To obtain cell integrals, each density is multiplied by its own $\Delta E_{\mathrm{avail}}\Delta W$, including the full width of each tail bin. The projection applies the fine-grid mask and uses no additional acceptance factor at the destination binning; whether one is needed has not been decided. The deposit's README gives the array ordering and a worked projection check.

inputs/appA_methods.tex:145:\paragraph{Sixteen-cell closure at held-out tunes.} The study scores $\Csub$ with and without the neutron and proton transport bands. The separate Poisson and noise-free mean-bias checks stay within their criteria at these tunes (Sec.~\ref{sec:closure-interval-diagnostics}). The joint interval shortfall leaves the relative contributions of bias and scatter unresolved.

    97	
    98	\subsection{Closure tests}
    99	\label{sec:muon-cross-section-validation}
   100	\label{sec:validation}
   101	
   102	All closure tests use the same detector simulation as the unfolding, and their conclusions apply to the specific injected shapes and amplitudes. The signal-only closure tests recover the injected $p_T$ and $p_\parallel$ features to median absolute fractional residuals of $0.046\%$ and $0.013\%$, respectively. These signal-only tests use five iterations and simulation from one run period. A $17.2\%$ Gaussian truth feature leaves a $1.69\%$ residual, 0.098 of the injection, which passes the feature-recovery check of Ref.~\cite{Canelli:2025guide}. Reweighting the pseudo-data by a quasielastic axial-mass variation or a flux variation gives residuals of $0.068\%$ and $0.376\%$, respectively, against references extrapolated from the accepted signal. These references probe shape changes conditional on the simulated acceptance. Table~\ref{tab:validation-inventory} lists the threshold applied to each check.
   103	
   104	A separate check of the effective response finds an approximately linear rise in the median signed fractional residual, from $0.57\%$ to $1.14\%$ and $3.43\%$ across the tested amplitudes (Table~\ref{tab:validation-inventory}). The check reweights reconstructed events as a function of the resolution variable $\Delta p_T=p_T^{\mathrm{reco}}-p_T^{\mathrm{true}}$ at fixed truth. The two momenta are classifier inputs at their respective levels, and their eventwise difference is outside the feature set. We assign no additional uncertainty from these closure residuals.
   105	
   106	The background treatment is tested separately with 20 pseudo-experiments at nominal truth, each unfolded with both subtraction methods. The two results differ in the mean by more than one statistical standard deviation in 14 of the 205 bins. In those bins, the purity-subtracted estimate deviates from the truth by as much as 5.9 statistical standard deviations, while the negative-weight estimate is within 0.44, using the purity-subtracted statistical block as a common scale (Appendix~\ref{app:negative-weight-background}).
   107	
   108	\phantomsection\label{sec:coverage-tunes} The joint regions fall short of the coverage criterion at both held-out GENIE v3 tunes. The tunes use SuSAv2 (\fvval{f1-tune-ar23}) and Valencia (\fvval{f1-tune-g18}) for two-particle--two-hole scattering. The joint regions contain the truth in \lrval{cov2d-ar23-joint} and \lrval{cov2d-g18-joint} of \lrval{cov2d-ntoys} pseudo-experiments, respectively. The initial score uses the covariance rebuilt at each tune with the prior-shape term and without the run-period block. The mean $\chi^2/\mathrm{ndf}$ is $\lrval{cov2d-ar23-chi2ndf}\pm\lrval{cov2d-ar23-chi2ndf-se}$ and $\lrval{cov2d-g18-chi2ndf}\pm\lrval{cov2d-g18-chi2ndf-se}$, respectively. Including the run-period block gives \fvval{rg-2d-ar23-full} and \fvval{rg-2d-g18-full}, still far above the expected value of about 1. The per-bin test attributes the coverage deficits in some bins to method bias (Appendix~\ref{app:closure-interval-diagnostics}). Coverage remains unvalidated for the reported bins, projections, and integral. These joint scores test combined covariances.     98	
    99	OmniFold estimates the truth distribution by iteratively reweighting simulated events with classifier-based likelihood ratios~\cite{Andreassen:2019cjw,Andreassen:2021zzk}. The first classifier distinguishes the background-corrected measured sample from selected simulated signal with the current weights. The odds $p/(1-p)$ for measured-class probability $p$ estimate the ratio of their weighted densities with the corresponding class normalization. We multiply each selected signal weight by this ratio and transfer it to the paired truth event. The second classifier compares the reweighted and original truth samples. Its likelihood ratio gives new weights relative to the original simulation. The matched detector-level records carry those weights into the next iteration.
   100	
   101	Truth signal events without a selected reconstructed partner enter the second step. At each iteration, a regressor trained with uniform weights on matched events predicts first-step weights for missed events from their truth observables. This corrects for selection efficiency using the simulated acceptance and assumes that the weights transfer from matched to missed events at the same truth inputs. At very low simulated efficiency the regressor extrapolates from matched events. We use five OmniFold iterations, with gradient-boosted-tree classifiers and regressor. The classifiers train and evaluate on the full samples without a held-out split, cross-fitting, or early stopping. The iteration count and learner capacity regularize the weights. The final weights can be integrated on any reporting grid.
   102	
   103	For the principal results, we give each data event unit weight and append the simulated background with the negative of its exposure-scaled weights. If $D(x)$ and $B(x)$ are the selected data and background densities in reconstructed coordinates $x$, the signed measured class targets the first-step ratio $[D(x)-B(x)]/S(x)$ where the net density is positive, with $S(x)$ the current weighted density of selected simulated signal.
   104	
   105	Before the first OmniFold classifier is trained, we convert the signed sample to nonnegative weights. A separate classifier, trained with absolute event weights, estimates the probability $g(x)$ that an event in the signed sample has positive weight. The original weight $w$ is replaced by $|w|\max\{0,2g(x)-1\}$~\cite{Nachman:2025staypositive}. The absolute-weight sample has density $D+B$. In the population limit $g=D/(D+B)$, so multiplying this density by the clipped factor gives the positive part of $D-B$. The learned factor implements this prescription without a subtraction grid. Some checks and diagnostics instead subtract the background with binned purity, weighting data by the estimated signal fraction in each reconstructed bin. We label these purity-subtracted (Appendix~\ref{app:negative-weight-background}).
   106	
   107	\subsection{Estimators and cross-section extraction}
   108	\label{sec:estimators-and-extraction}
   109	\label{sec:method-config}

     1	\section{Discussion and conclusions}
     2	\label{sec:discussion-and-conclusions}
     3	
     4	\subsection{What the measurements and tests establish}
     5	\label{sec:result-validity}
     6	
     7	We report the inclusive charged-current $\nu_\mu$ cross section in sixteen cells of the five-observable phase space. In the two-dimensional benchmark, the OmniFold central values reproduce MINERvA's published integral to about $1\%$. A correlated shape difference of undetermined origin remains. The five-observable central values preserve the normalization and agree with the lower-dimensional results in the tested projections. The closure tests reveal residual method bias that the reported covariances do not fully account for. At nominal truth, the negative-weight background subtraction removes most of the bias of purity subtraction in the affected muon bins and, in a separate test, at high $W$. A post hoc check finds each coarse-grid generator-test rejection also visible in a matched projection, so the tests show no evidence of discrimination beyond those projections. Table~\ref{tab:validity} gives the scope of each result.
     8

    25	
    26	\subsection{Limitations and their mechanisms}
    27	\label{sec:multidimensional-limitations}
    28	\label{sec:limitations}
    29	\label{sec:execsummary-limitations}
    30	
    31	Agreement in the total rate and marginal projections leaves recovery of the joint five-observable distribution unresolved. A shift of cross section between hadronic cells within a muon quadrant cancels in the quadrant sum. In the five-axis $W$ injection test, the $W$ marginal met its recovery criterion while the joint $(E_{\mathrm{avail}},W)$ cells in the peak interval fell below their targets.
    32	
    33	Recovery of correlations depends on how much information the measured inputs carry about truth. The other inputs fix reconstructed $W$. Separating events in true $W$ therefore relies on the simulated mapping from reconstructed variables to truth. The missed-event correction depends on simulated acceptance. It transfers weights learned on selected events to missed events at the same truth inputs and relies on extrapolation at very low acceptance (Sec.~\ref{sec:omnifold-implementation}).
    34	
    35	The sixteen-cell result is sensitive to regularization and to the starting distribution. It retains part of the prior at both the GiBUU-like truth and the synthetic $q_3$ tilt, and its cell integrals differ from those of binned iterative Bayesian unfolding on the same inputs. In a study on noise-free samples, the retention at the GiBUU hybrid is consistent with a limit set by the weights of the missed events, and at the $q_3$ tilt with a limit set by the representation of the second, truth-level step (Sec.~\ref{sec:fived16-prr}). Coarsening the grid leaves appreciable prior dependence. Rounding-level changes also move the central values beyond the tolerance of the stability test.
    36	
    37	\phantomsection
    38	\label{sec:usage}\label{sec:limitations-use}
    39	The covariances propagate the specified variations but are too small for the deviations of held-out-tune pseudo-experiments from the truth (Sec.~\ref{sec:closure-interval-diagnostics}). The scale of the run-period term and its ability to account for bias from pooling periods require independent checks. Linear projections of reported bins inherit the limitations of the input covariance. For inference on new observables, treating the unfolded events as independent in a likelihood can understate uncertainty because the fit correlates their weights.
    40	
    41	\subsection{Implications for multidimensional measurements}
    42	\label{sec:unfolding-outlook}
    43	\label{sec:summary}
    44	
    45	Validation of the reported joint distribution requires demonstrating recovery and uncertainty coverage at the reporting resolution, including tests that vary correlations (Sec.~\ref{sec:closure-interval-diagnostics}). The origins of the run-period step and the 2D shape difference with MINERvA remain unknown. The transfer of weights to events missed by the selection still needs validation.
    46

   270	The prespecified substitution study in Sec.~\ref{sec:fived16-prr} tested which parts of the procedure contribute to prior retention. The study replaced one part of the procedure at a time on the same noise-free samples, with the iterations continued to \fvval{t3-kmax}, where $\rho$ levels off. At the GiBUU hybrid, with no part replaced, $\rho$ is \fvval{t3-rho-b0-gibuu}. Giving the events that fail the selection their true weights lowers $\rho$ to \fvval{t3-rho-mo-gibuu}, about the value reached when every event receives its true weight before the second step. No data analysis has either oracle input. Letting the missed events keep their previous weights instead of the regressor's prediction, weighting the regressor, or removing the background with its refinement each changes $\rho$ by less than \fvval{t3-dmax-gibuu}, against a threshold of \fvval{prr-floor}. The retention there is consistent with a limit set by the weights of the missed events. At the $q_3$ tilt, with no part replaced, $\rho$ is \fvval{t3-rho-b0-q3}, and no replacement lowers $\rho$ by \fvval{prr-floor}. Even with true weights for every event, $\rho=\fvval{t3-rho-op-q3}$ remains after the second, truth-level step, consistent with a limit set by that step's representation. At both truths the result matches the pseudo-data at reconstruction level but not at truth level, consistent with directions the reconstructed inputs constrain weakly.
